ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e3869	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e3885	Mr President, we have just discussed this in depth – with the aim of becoming faster, more efficient and, of course, more favourable in terms of management. This is achieved by the fact that we primarily abolish double-tracks and triple-tracks. In the medium term, we expect a savings potential of €8 to €10 million, but I say that we also need a start-up funding in the first and second years of the reform – in the first year, in 2013, €1.5 million, in the second year – but we manage to eliminate double-tracks and triple-tracks. Especially in the field of human synergies, we manage to save €2.5 million already in 2014, up to 2016 even €3.5 million. The same applies, of course, to the area of resources. In the administration, speaking in the field of logistics and infrastructure, we also save 2.6 to 3.1 million € annually. Above all, the Supreme Law is that a task should be put into one hand. I can clearly explain this to the criminal police and security police sector, where there have been several criminal police departments so far. I think in particular of Carinthia, where a criminal police department was to be found in the security department, in the state police command and in two federal police departments. In the new structure, there will only be a criminal police department in the future. So, of course, it will be in other areas, and this will save us in about €1.5 million. That is, we will bring this €8 to €10 million through a modern structure.

And by limiting interest rates also for new members of the pension fund system, reductions are countered. This concerns the so-called illusory promises I spoke about earlier. We hope that the new law will make this much better. You have also said critically, Mr Öllinger: Who then represents the respective entitled ones? – With this innovation, there is now the possibility that the authorised will be represented in the Supervisory Boards. The maturity period is reduced from five to three years. And there is the possibility that you will continue to pay fully into the pension fund, although perhaps the employment relationship has changed. Just think of karence, motherhood, paternal care, short-term work or part-time! In all of these employment conditions, the pension fund will be able to continue to be operated on a variable basis, so that it does not lead to a reduction in the pension. Overall, I am convinced that this innovation was urgently needed and will bring improvements, but it will not yet be the last reform for this system of the second pillar. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e8514	Mr President. In case of all legitimate criticism: Let us take a look at the situation in Austria! The introduction of the pension fund system in 1990 led to a very strong upturn in the beginning. The returns were relatively high for all, surprisingly. The crisis in particular contributed to the fact that this was not the case, because of the turbulence in the financial markets, of course, pension funds have not been spared, even though our cash offices had been relatively conservative. I can understand very well that the Austrians are disappointed, frustrated and largely insecure. The groups concerned, namely the social partners and the pensioners, have also drawn up appropriate improvements, and these have been largely incorporated into this legislative change. What is now being improved? – The competition between pension funds, as has already been said. And what makes competition? – Competition creates a correspondingly better price. Competition also creates improved quality and better service. What are we gonna do? – We also create competition against other precautionary products. And there is also greater transparency. Customers need to be informed more clearly and in more detail. And there is also the possibility of a six percent deductibility of the tax. So, in general, a good law. Of course, an improvement is always possible, but it is the right way, because it ensures that we can live independently and financially in the last third of our lives. Thank you.

I think it is worthwhile to fight this for the reasons mentioned, but that is what we must be public about, because it is the defence of banking secrecy alone, when it comes to potential foreign taxpayers, that will not be possible. And then the question still arises whether it must necessarily be the case that the inner Austrian banking secret, if one believes, as it were, what this is to create – but be it – is already at risk, because we are preparing to enter into the data exchange in exchange with the countries of the Union, but possibly other third countries, when it comes to these tax foreigners, who have a reason why they are coming to Austria. So there is something we have to think of, because I am not right that the Austrian representation at EU level occurs in such a way that this is defended with – I quote literally – “with teeth and claws”, but no higher idea of transparency and justice at all takes place. There has been a great deal of success in the last three years. We have rebuilt institutions, we have regulated the capital market much more intensively, we have helped Portugal and Ireland and are confident that this aid has also benefited and that it can refinance itself within a medium-term period. We have significantly deepened economic policy coordination in several steps, keyword: Six Pack, Fiscal Pact, Two Pack. We have set up a temporary macro-financial shield, which we have now converted with the EFSF into a permanent system and will then be permanent with the ESM. We have also established the basis for the fact that there is a network of authorities across Europe that are equally responsible for financial market supervision. Yesterday, the EU's Finance Ministers agreed on this uniform supervision, in the framework of the package which we have now forwarded to the European Parliament as a compromise with Basel III, in order to vote with Parliament. The financial market situation is somewhat more stable, although not sufficiently stable. But we recognise that there is more budgetary discipline throughout the euro area and also across the European Union and that this has improved. Furthermore, instability in Greece is a huge challenge. At present, for example, we are missing contact persons who have a majority in Parliament. It is currently very difficult to discuss its problems with Greece because the existing expert government is no longer in office. This is not the case with us, where the old government remains until a new government is there, but it is a new expert government. [[Abg. Petzner: You suffer from total loss of reality! I will not give him any consent! We understand that third countries should also comply with these directives and that the basis of assessment should be extended in this respect. I gave him a rejection. Now this directive is coming into the Council, and I am convinced that our Federal Chancellor will also take this position. Thank you.

Point Report of the Financial Committee on the report of the Federal Minister for Finance on an operator-independent player card on the basis of the resolution of the National Council of 16 December 1989 on the financial situation in the European Union. ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e11945	We're now on the 5th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Dr. Stummvoll spoke. 5 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e11972	Mr President. Mrs Finanzminister! High house! After these high-speed flights from Europe to the general financial situation, we come back to an Austrian problem, namely, gambling law. I should like to remind you that one and a half years ago, in modernising the gambling law, we gave the highest priority to protecting players and preventing gambling. That was very important to us. We have incorporated a large number of provisions into this new law, which has been taken into account in this priority. I'm just mentioning a few examples. We have enormously limited the number of machines. We have introduced ID checks. We have introduced a minimum age. We have all the machines connected to the Bundesrechenzentrum. This means we have the overall overview and total control. We have also anchored the possibility of player locks in automatic salons, in video lotteries and so on. We have introduced our own financial police force, which, I believe, has already seized some 1,700 illegal vending machines. – A wealth of measures that all aim to strengthen player protection and prevent gambling. We have set up our own staff office in the Ministry of Finance – the head, Dr. Kohl, is also present today – especially with this task: Player protection and prevention against gambling. Ladies and gentlemen, of course I know that there are also opinions in social democracy: The best way to protect players would be to ban gambling at all. But all the experiences we have worldwide show that then other strategies take place, such as fleeing in intoxicated backrooms, fleeing abroad – in Austria there is hardly any location that is more than an hour away from abroad – or fleeing to the ever-increasing, non-concessional internet gambling game. To be honest, I have to say that it is preferable to me, it is not forbidden and we are making very, very strict rules, because of course we do not want the gambling to lead to human tragedies, to high social costs and that the threshold to crime is also getting lower. That's not a question at all: If one of the game seekers no longer has money, then the transition to crime is of course a very short one. That is to say, in the law itself, we have put in place a wealth of measures to ensure this objective. In addition, we have asked the Ministry of Finance to present a report on whether the instrument of an operator-independent player card would also be a measure to act in this direction, and this report is available to us today. It is an excellent report – I really thank you for it; we also discussed it in detail in the Financial Committee – which also shows, among other things, that it is ultimately also about the acceptance of this idea by the players themselves. All the forced measures – the report says very clearly – are exactly what I said earlier about the ban. They will lead to an escape in backrooms, abroad or to the Internet. That is, we have the problem that we are probably not reaching the goal with forced measures, but very much through information and information. One problem in this report is that we have few international examples. The report says that there are examples in Norway and in a province in Canada, otherwise not very many. But there is a study that proves how much this protection is necessary for the player himself. The discrepancy between the player's assessment of how much he plays and how long he plays, and reality is huge. For example, the study has shown that the actual loss, the actual use of money at such games, is seven times as high as the player himself estimates, and the time as long as he plays is actually three times as high as he himself estimates it. So in that respect, this player card is also a kind of self-protection for the player, who thereby also gets information about his corresponding behavior in gambling. Again: That seems to me to be a very important point. We must achieve, through a wealth of measures that are being considered at this staff station in the financial sector, that the players accept this instrument themselves, as self-protection, as protection against themselves. However, there is one area – I have also cut it in the Financial Committee – where we do not yet know the solution, and that is the distinction between those we want to protect and Austria as a tourist country. Of course, we do not want us to drive people into illegality, that we drive people into social ruin, but on the other hand, the question arises: Why should rich Arab Sheikhs, why should rich oligarchs from Russia not have the chance to spend their money in Austria, so to speak, to our Minister for Finance? [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: If it's not a black money! If it's not a Mafia money!]] In this area, a demarcation is likely to be very difficult, so this is an area that we need to focus more on in the future: How can we find a meaningful demarcation? I am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that we are taking another important step in this report and that further steps will have to be taken so that Austria can continue to expand its pioneer role – and I say we have a pioneer role in Europe – in the field of player protection and game-seeking prevention. Thank you.

We do not hope, however, that there will be a distortion of competition on the grounds that illegal, perhaps even illegal, or other illegal calls for tenders would be the most embarrassing thing for Austria, Mrs. Minister, if there were to be complaints against calls for tenders and these actions would result at European level – and you know how this is strictly handled there, especially in the competition of gambling providers – that a competition authority may take up such calls and that we will get into chaos. We don't want to. We stand for responsibility in the sense of player protection, but also for a free market in this area, so that in the end not someone who should not be preferred is preferred.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e13647	Madam President. Members of the Federal Government! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! In fact, in our federalism, many decisions take a long time – and I too long – especially where there is a division of competences. If you are talking about efficiency and improvement, you also need to be aware that the compromise has taken too long in the past. And there would be no doubt to mention some questions as examples. But if you look at our state structure – state, federalism, developed federal states – you also have to see that it strengthens in the population – not less, but stronger! – the interest in shaping, participating in, knowing people who represent one, and also in increasing the need to be politically involved. If one does not meet these needs and if people do not also try to get involved regionally, feel that there is someone with whom they are negotiating directly, talking, discussing, fighting, dealing with, then, I believe, the gap between the population and what is often called political starkness or what appears to be in lower elections will become even greater. So a meaningful organization that structurally improves the strengths of our country in federalism through strong communities and countries and in cooperation with the federal government, but which also takes into account the need for the population – certainly even smaller communities – to be close to politics and to political processes and political decisions would be in the interest of all. I have often experienced in the past that reforms have not taken so long because there are too many communities or because federalism is too difficult with the national structures, but because the internal processes that shape our democracy are so fragmented. [[Abg. Bucher: We don't know these problems! Of course, you can also invest money in the administration, but in reality there is nothing to do with it, but – on the contrary! – you need to see where you can get something and where you can improve the state in its function, but also make it more efficient. That is why I am very glad that very specific points have been put forward here, and then we can discuss them. That is definitely something worth noting. I'd love to do that here, too. We have these points, I would like to address them essentially. Parliament. – That is quite right, since I am entirely of the opinion of the applicants, because we increasingly have a ministerial democracy or a ministerial state. There is now a completely uneven issue because the Ministries have the possibilities of their entire legal services. With their legal services, they have much more resources, a large number of officials and this for a much longer period of time. These officials are, in fact, presenting in broad areas in this state how this development continues. This is a wrong situation against which we, as legislators, must very well defend ourselves as Parliament. This also means that Parliament will be equipped with certain possibilities so that we can resist at all, because if we want to make a proposal, we want to launch a legislative initiative, then it is really self-taught in a way. Then we must take care of ourselves, perhaps in our clubs trying to build up competence, but there is no real support from Parliament. I am a hundred percent of the opinion expressed, because I believe that this would also be a very important process in terms of the sharing of powers. [[Complaints with FPÖ and BZÖ.]] The President of the Federal Republic of Germany is to be abolished. – I don’t think that’s right. He is directly elected, and that already has a certain symbolic power, even if you may not always be happy with the people. This is just like this in a democracy, but there is in itself the fact that there are the directly elected representatives, who in some way already represent the state outside and who also has a few tasks. I don't think that makes sense. I wouldn't get rid of it personally. This office is an institution in that State, which has a special meaning by direct elections. National Council elects government. – This is then an outflow of the fact that there are no Federal Presidents, I suppose, otherwise I will not see any real improvement here if it does. We believe that there are ten ministers or ten members of the government, no matter how much they want to divide. We already said that. Since a streamlining is carried out, it makes sense. It should be essentially ministers, therefore ten ministries, i.e. a streamlining. This is absolutely right, even here, of course, the approach – I also give the applicants the right thing – is that they are going through it and then streamlining it. [[Complaints with FPÖ and BZÖ.]] Land days and state governments shall be abolished. – This is a question of the debate on the distribution of competence in itself. You can bite your teeth out very easily, except you say there is only one legislator and everything else you can do. Otherwise, of course, it's very difficult to figure this out. I am of the opinion that it makes sense, in the present situation at least, to have days of land, because they are in some way an identification area for the countries. And the different countries – as we always come to them at all other points somewhere else – have a certain historical form. This is a question of culture, also of the concept of home and the immediate sense of home, but it is to a certain extent also a form of competition. You know exactly how people identify with their country and then are proud of it when certain things work better there than elsewhere. I'm thinking of an example now. We have also always talked about the merger of health insurance funds. If everything had expired according to the pattern of the Vienna regional health insurance fund, we would now be worse there if we had not seen that the Upper Austrian regional health insurance fund or the Tyrolean CCC could have a positive balance, while the Wiener always have a rich minus. Because of this one could at least prove that it is different. Of course, there are other things that have played along, of course, but it is also a question of competition, and that is why countries... – I just said it. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Bartenstein.]] I didn't say now that you don't have to put this together in the long run, but I'm just saying there's always interesting clues when you watch it. At least once, when it comes to land days, it is also an identification of the countries and also a possibility to compete here, which can ultimately also benefit the state. Head of State directly. – Yes, this is certainly an approach that we have always represented, but that would contradict it if we abolished the land days. That said Mr Cap, that is also my opinion that this would logically not fit in. The mayor is directly elected. – This too has proved to be a good thing. There is, of course, the situation that mayors then have no majority in the council, but that is only a sign of the mayor's personality. If he persists and if he also has to deal with the majority in the council and yet does his things, it is good. We also have such examples in our group, where this works very well. That is quite a sensible approach. Here too, we believe that this is a good thing. More direct democracy. . We debated this for a long time yesterday. It's our opinion, too. In the whole debate on the attitude of the people to politics, political starryness and so on, part of this is certainly that the population is increasingly perceived as not being allowed to decide on essential issues, that decisions will continue to be taken away from it, up to the European Union and that they have less and less to do with it. This is one point of approach. And the second point is that there must be a certain correctiveness to what is being raised here in Parliament by the elected representatives, with the essential questions not to be determined by Parliament, but by the initiators of a popular desire, which ultimately leads to a referendum or by those who then have a veto vote. Not we as Parliament then have the right to present these issues. So here I am not the opinion that has already been represented. We do not have the right to take restrictive action and to say that we must vote on it, not on it, and there is now a catalogue, but, as we have already debated, essentially what is happening in Parliament will be able to vote on the issues that can be voted on in Parliament. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We have presented a very clear system here, the three-stage model, which also works in other countries, where Parliament is once presented with the initiative, Parliament can then implement it, and only if Parliament does not, will it continue and the referendum will be established. These are all useful things. On the other hand, one should not stop, and above all it should not be made impossible, as is now unfortunately done, with the number of people who have to sign such a popular desire. Let us think, for example, of the educational people's desire! With 460,000 signatures, you would never have been able to force a referendum by your model of 700,000 or 850,000 signatures. If that's a popular desire that doesn't make it, who else could do it? Therefore, please make realistic figures possible! Finally, the electorate would have to decide. We have made a proposal, we have said 250 000 signatures, which corresponds to 4 percent of the electorate. So much is needed to be elected to Parliament. That is our approach, but it must not be so that it is impossible to do so and only apparently creates the possibility of direct democracy. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Unite voting dates. – That’s something I mean, it doesn’t make sense, because if there are really serious changes like the splitting of a party, for example, or the like, then it may be necessary to re-elect. [[Abg. Hornek: What are you up to?]] - You mean we're so big now. We'd rather suck up, so that's going in the opposite direction. We're very inclusive. Punctual administration, clear competences. – This is, of course, a very important issue. The speaking time has passed, of course we could debate it for hours. Financial control would also be important, it would be a simple step to improve financial control. We know that it is not even possible to carry out an orderly control. Firstly, the Court of Auditors does not have the competence to control smaller communities, but it would be useful to have an overall view and a comparison. I don't understand why you're blocking. Considering communities with fewer than 10,000 citizens would also make sense to improve financial control. All of these are sensible approaches. There's a lot to do. The devil is in detail, and I hope it doesn't go out as often as it's called, it's always been, it's got to stay like this and win the blockers.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e17352	Mr Singer. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to speak on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party and of - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e17374	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I am dealing with the Court of Auditors' report on control in the Federal Schools. This report was prepared in the winter of 2009/2010 and I am allowed to draw up a few points that the Court of Auditors has identified. On the one hand, he criticized the basic understanding of controlling. There are no uniform, binding requirements for a controlling system, and this, of course, leads to different perceptions of the controlling tasks between the Ministry and the State School Councils. And he also criticises the lack of the target and performance agreements between the management and the administration. That is to say, the policy-strategic requirements are not present. The Court also criticises the responsibilities of the Court of Auditors, namely that the controlling tasks take place in 14 departments or a superior body, and the Court of Auditors' recommendation is to set up a staff station. Also, from my point of view, the IT support was rightly criticized. There are a total of seven different software products, and this clearly follows the Court's request for a single software. The opinion on data collection and use is also interesting. The Court of Auditors writes that there are double messages from schools and that no necessary reports have been received and that the data on the student administration are also not available online. Of course, in this case too, the Court of Auditors' request for appropriate access to the data is understandable in order to ensure a better resolution. Ladies and gentlemen! 90 percent of the funds for federal schools are for personnel costs, and therefore, from my point of view, the personal controlling is of the same importance. And there are very, very different approaches, and that is why the Court of Auditors' call for uniform procedures to be made in this case is understandable. In the field of education control, the Court of Auditors positively assesses the quality management system for secondary and higher schools and recommends the introduction of this system in the AHS area, where this quality assurance management system was still lacking at the time of the review. In summary, ladies and gentlemen: There are a number of recommendations from the Court of Auditors in this report. The Ministry of Education has said that these recommendations are being taken very seriously, and it is also working very intensively with the cooperation of the Court of Auditors on a general understanding of control. I hope that the Court of Auditors' proposals will be implemented soon, in accordance with the Government Agreement, in which this controlling system is properly anchored. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00157_d7e18643	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors. High house! What is this PPP model about? – It is simply the partnership between the public and the private. The objective is also quite clear, and I only recall that the financing, production and operation of various roads – in this case the North motorway A 5 and the Vienna bypass S 1 and S 2 – should be transferred to private partners and therefore the risks should also be borne. The goals of the federal government to choose a PPP model were quite clear: higher rate of implementation – that is, a faster completion of this project –, financing outside the budget – an essential aspect –, precise definition of the project structures, acquisition of benchmarks, efficiency improvement and, of course, forcing competition. The fact is that the Court of Auditors has, of course, set itself the goal of examining the strengths and weaknesses of this new model; Risk assessment, remuneration model, quality assurance and effectiveness, project building and process organisation, public authorities and procurement procedures – at that time still a project, as it finally looked out, I cannot say today, of funds of € 993 million that have come to bear here. A small wormhead drop today: At that time the Court of Auditors had already examined, i.e. in July/October 2008 and until February 2009; the Committee took place in June 2011, and today, in May 2012, we finally have this report here in plenary. The Court of Auditors' criticism is obvious: Benchmarks can only be applied to sub-areas of the PPP project, the need for cost optimization in the transmission of risk, and overall no serious – and that is the essential point: no serious! – advantages over conventional procurement. That's also the key statement. Whether or not the establishment of a road network with PPP models was more cost-effective could not be determined simply because of the data available to the Court of Auditors at that time. Of course, the PPP model also clearly shows that there were no serious advantages in the procurement for the ASFINAG and thus also for the public. At the time, the ASFINAG calculated an economic advantage of € 167.97 million for the award of the PPP property model Eastregion. This has been assessed by the Court of Auditors in this way: “This is a value of subjective probability which, because of its inherent bandwidth” – I read this so – “can also adversely develop the cost estimates and evaluations of risks to the contractor for the public.” So say: The Court of Auditors did not really believe this at the time. The award procedures, according to the Court of Auditors, have been transparently dealt with, but in the conventional sense, the duration of the procedure is usually between five and eight months, and the PPP model has lasted three times that long, namely 24 months. The Court of Auditors also clearly criticises the high advisory costs of at least €12.52 million. These are advisory costs from external consulting companies. Here you could have used your own resources in the house of the ASFINAG and it could have been much cheaper and certainly just as professional. The Court further criticises that the ASFINAG for agricultural land used for road construction has at least paid more than € 115 million – around double the traffic values set out in the Principle Assessments. The areas agreed in the concession contract have led to a realising pressure – it had to be decided quickly and built – and this was quite clear when you were under pressure at land purchases, which means that you have to pay a higher redemption price, and this has become striking here. In the case of public and private enterprises PPP projects, the risks are shared on private partners, but ultimately, if this project is taken over in 2039, the federal government will also have to bear the costs of renovation, because it has not been agreed at the time and has been determined in a contractual way, who will actually stand for the corresponding renovation and renovation measures. I say thank you, Mr President, and thank you very much to your team. Once again, you have done quite an excellent job and I would like to see the Court of Auditors involved in such large projects, but also in small ones, from the outset, in order to support them in a accompanying and controllable manner. In fact, it is the content of the Convention on Human Rights that is of a kind. 8, namely the right to respect for private and family life, the right to data protection can be clearly deduced. It is also true that a fundamental right to data protection is guaranteed in data protection law. Both are in the constitutional rank and are undisputed. But then it is absolutely impossible to understand why such an application should not be dealt with in the Human Rights Committee, nor should it be dealt with in terms of content, because the Human Rights Committee has been set up precisely for such initiatives, namely for initiatives in the field of human rights and fundamental rights. And neither do we have the Human Rights Committee in this legislative period, but it has been for years. And even in such applications, the argument would probably be: Well, since we no longer have the death penalty in Austria, it is actually about foreign policy, the abolition of the death penalty elsewhere in the world. Of course it can't be! If both the Convention on Human Rights and the Austrian Fundamental Law provide a fundamental right to data protection – which is currently not guaranteed – then the Human Rights Committee is exactly the place where such initiatives should be discussed. We will repeatedly address this narrowed human rights understanding, because the Human Rights Committee cannot treat virtually any matter because of this very shortened human rights understanding. Most oppositional applications do not see the plenary session of the National Council because they are routinely postponed by the government coalitions. We will therefore not agree to the allocation to the Constitutional Committee. Heinz-Christian Strache will never hide behind immunity! We will not take this political link, if he is aware, to say that he must not be extradited or we do not agree to the persecution. We would ask, of course, to clarify this and to see who is behind the fact that the persecution is agreed – but on the basis of a law-compliant decision that must be voiced: political context, yes. Thank you.
